  • How draft 2 was made

Tauheed, Bab 4 «وحدانیت»: hub plan, draft 2

20 lessons (draft 1 had 25). Every lesson opens and closes: tap a title. Inside, The steps is open; the rest is folded. Source refs are hidden; the Refs button shows them. Comment by selecting text, or with a section's button.

new taught here first · ↺ B3 S16 partly taught there; the new part is taught here · ↺ B1 S03 taught there; one-line reminder here · core repeat · ask kept in full, you decide · ask for you · Claude's judgement an import kept on Claude's framing

Decide first

  1. B4-A01 (new form), 20 lessons with the five folds above. Options the readers saw and did not recommend: S07 into S06; S10 (truth is one, 7 points) into S07 or S09; S01 into S02. Long folds: S04 (33 points, 13 steps), S11 (11 steps), S17 (10 steps), S19 (11 steps); each entry proposes which steps could go to a short closing section.
  2. Rule 7 against rule 1. Some points Bab 1 and 2 borrowed from Bab 4 are judged SAME or PART (U4.29-19, U4.5-1 to -7, -9, -13, U4.4-1 to -3, U4.7-20, U4.22-1 to -3, U4.29-3, -5, U4.32-2, U4.7-6, U4.12-13). Draft 2 keeps them in full here (Bab 4 is their home), flagged. Recommended: keep in full here, as the rule for borrowed points says.
  3. The shared-Arabic table missed texts. The three readers found by a vowel-free search about 23 texts printed in full in Bab 1 to 3 that OVERLAP section 4 does not list (for example 112:1 in B2 S01 and B2 S09; the adna report in B1 S03; 4:48 in B2 S10; 51:56 in B1 S02 and B1 S17); they are counted in the 45 and flagged in the entries. Two table matches are only shared wording, and one table row (U4.29-3 P058:8-9) is a shared clause in a different narration; those are printed normally. Recommended: the table check is rerun with a looser match before more lessons are written.
  4. One verdict looks wrong: U4.7-2 (S08, Kafi 1:69 «دعامة الإنسان العقل») is NONE in the check, but B2 S06 prints the same text and draws the same teaching. Recommended: treat as SAME (recall).
  5. Draft-1 A02 to A05 and A11 carry over, renumbered: A02 S01 its own lesson; A03 S12 its own lesson (written); A04 U4.31 as S16 to S18 (three lessons now, after the S17 fold); A05 shared homes as drawn; A11 the M2 and M3 Fasls left for the M2 hub. Rule 7 closes some draft-1 options ("move to Bab 1 or 2"), so those Asks are reworded in S01 and S02.

The 20 lessons

توحید دین کا پہلا اصل ہے: ہر نبی ایک ہی پکار لایا، رسول اللہ صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ نے لا الہ الا اللہ کہلوایا، ائمہ علیہم السلام اور اہلِ ایمان نے اس کی قیمت دی، اور امام مہدی علیہ السلام کے ہاتھوں یہ کلمہ ساری زمین پر پہنچے گا۔

1. The principle and the call S01 to S05

S01 توحید دین کا پہلا اصل ہے اور تمام انبیاء کی دعوت اسی کے گرد گھومتی ہے

draft 1: H01 · 2 new · 9 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Why did the prophets come? Not mainly to prove God but to make people knowers and worshippers of the One (یکتاشناس، یکتاپرست); if oneness stands, God stands. (EZ P038:2-9, P039:1-4, P040:1-9; U4.1-13). ↺ B2 S06 · B3 S01Already taught: B2 S06 "the prophets did not come mainly to argue for God's existence" (with B3 S01: existence and oneness are separate questions),New here: tawhid as the axis of their work, tying every existent's thread to God, in EZ P038:2-5, P039:2-4, P040:4-6
  2. How does the Quran say "one"? wahid, ahad, «لا اله الا هو»; "He is God, One" is a definite principle; the Quran stresses tawhid more than we imagine, and tawhid means ascribing oneness to the proven self-subsisting Creator and believing it. (JS P001:4-9, P002:6-10, P014:4-8, P017:1-8; U4.1-1, -6, -7). [U4.1-1 PART: recall B3 S01 "oneness is treated right after proving the Maker", then new: oneness as one of the Quran's most important attributes, in JS P001:4-9, P002:6-10 | U4.1-6 NEW | U4.1-7 PART: recall B1 S09 "tawhid is central in the Quran", then new: the definition, in JS P017:3-8]
  3. What does "one" mean? No partner, no likeness, no division; counted a positive attribute (we ascribe it) and a negative one (it denies partner, likeness, division). (JS P018:1-2, P002:11-12, P004:22-30; U4.1-8). ↺ B3 S01Already taught: B3 S01 "al-Rida: God is made one and not divided",New here: the positive and negative classing, in JS P018:1-2
  4. How much must everyone know? (JS P003:1-17, P004:2-30; U4.1-2; TC MM P026, U4.3-9). [U4.1-2 SAME: recall only, "Recall B1 S03: the lowest knowing, per the al-Fath b. Yazid report, is to confess there is no god but He, with no likeness and no peer"; JS's repeat under the recall, flagged ASK | U4.3-9 PART (Teacher Copy): recall B1 S03 and B1 S10 "the minimum is confessing no god but Him; God makes Himself known", then new: Ali's line in Kitab Sulaym, the least by which one is a believer, in MM P026:3, P026:8-9]
  5. Where does it stand in religion? First: asked "teach me faith" (the Malik ibn Nuwayra setting), the Prophet put oneness first, then the resurrection, his prophethood, Ali's wilaya, then the obligations; no ruling is asked before the testimony. (JS P005:3-15, P006:8-9, P007:8-16; EZ P009:1-18, P010:1-15; U4.1-3; TC JS P043:1-10, U4.1-16). [U4.1-3 PART: recall B3 S01 "asked to summarise faith, the Prophet named the Lord's oneness first", then new: the Malik setting and the full order, in JS P005:3-15, P007:13-15, EZ P009:10-15, P010:9-15 | U4.1-16 PART (Teacher Copy): recall B1 S13 "felicity depends on the basic roots", then new: the testimony formula (God possessing all good, free of all evil, One) and the five-item list, in JS P043:3-10]
  6. How weighty is it? A daruri of Islam, so its denier is outside Islam. (EZ P015:3-4, P015:23, P027:5-17; no ledger point, taught as draft 1; U4.1-9). [U4.1-9 SAME (Teacher Copy): recall only, "Recall B1 S14: Khoei (Misbah al-Faqaha) counts four pillars, God, His oneness, prophethood, bodily resurrection"; JS's repeat under the recall, flagged ASK]
  7. So what is it for us? The most binding obligation; shirk is the wrong God does not forgive (4:48), yet one sincere testimony after sixty years of shirk owes no qada or kaffara. (EZ P037:9-21; U4.1-12). new
  8. Close: is knowing oneness a value in itself, even for one who "has no business with God"? Left open as the hook to the next lessons. (EZ P046:1-19; U4.1-19). ↺ B1 S10Already taught: B1 S10 "knowing God is required even without practical need",New here: the question put to oneness ("one or ten, what difference?"), in EZ P046:13-19
Asks for Ali 18
  1. ASK (B4-A01, now): the chat's proposal of 20 lessons after the 2026-11-19 rules (folds H05 into H04, H13 into H12, H17 into H16, H21 into H20, H24 into H23). This lesson is not folded.
  2. ASK (B4-A02): S01 as its own lesson on JS and EZ, or folded into the head of S02 (MM) or H09. Recommend: own lesson. With U4.1-2 and U4.1-9 now recalls, the lesson is shorter; still a lesson.
  3. U4.1-2 SAME: teach JS's repeat again under the recall, or recall only (see Repeats). Recommend recall only, with the B1 S03 block reprinted and flagged.
  4. U4.1-9 SAME: teach JS's Khoei passage again under the recall, or recall only. Recommend recall only; draft-1 Ask 5 (one line naming the book, Arabic in Urdu only, or drop) is replaced by this.
  5. Speaker of the adna report: Imam Rida (Uyun) or Imam Kazim (Kafi 1:86); Jami has one report from «أبي الحسن» and does not settle it. Now only for the reprinted block; follow B1 S03's choice.
  6. Malik b. Nuwayra story: print it in Jami's wording (two testimonies, pillars, wilaya), or replace with the MM "four" report (U4.1-3).
  7. The sixty-year mushrik: write the inference, and print 4:48 alone in its Quran wording (U4.1-12).
  8. Close on "is knowing oneness a value in itself" as an open hook, or drop it (U4.1-19).
  9. U4.1-14 held (taqlid and the Pope): Bab 18, or keep in this Bab's Teacher Copy. Bab 1 is no longer open to it (rule 7: no borrowing back).
  10. U4.1-4 moved to H10: confirm, or does U4.1 keep the "four" report. Note: its text («لا يؤمن عبد حتى يؤمن بأربعة») is already printed in full in B2 S09; wherever it lands it is reprinted and flagged.
  11. Leave out 16:90 with U4.1-11, and U4.1-15 with 1:2, or keep at H23/H24.
  12. 33:39 with U4.1-17 to H10, or leave out; trim Kafi 8:195 to «ومن البر التوحيد» at H16.
  13. Doctrine: wilaya as root and tawhid as branch (P048:17-18, P049:10, P050), moved to H16; how to reconcile with "tawhid the first principle".
  14. P015:7-9 and P015:18-22 (non-daruri examples, Shimr): Handout or Teacher Copy; P016 s10-23 restore or not; P037:1-8 lead-in.
  15. P039:3 «خدایان»: render so the Urdu does not read as endorsing many gods; confirm omitted remainders stay out.
  16. Adna wording: print Jami's «ولا شبه له ولا نظير», not JS's «لا شبيه له، لا نظير له» (matches B1 S03's block).
  17. U4.3-9 pointer: correct «فيقز» to «فيقر», and give «هو الإيمان» on 48:4 to Imam al-Sadiq as al-Kafi has it.
  18. New: four texts here were missed by the shared-Arabic table as "printed in full" (the adna report in B1 S03, 112:1 in B2 S01 and B2 S09, 4:48 in B2 S10, the «عرفني نفسك» dua in B1 S10). Recommend the table check be rerun with a looser match (from the first word of the block, and on re-read texts) before Bab 4 is written. Settled by the 2026-11-19 rules and dropped: none of the draft-1 Asks is fully settled; draft-1 Ask 1 (25 or 15) is restated as Ask 1, draft-1 Ask 5 is replaced by Ask 4.
Taught before in Bab 1 to 3 12
  • SAME (recalled, not taught): U4.1-2 (the lowest knowing): "Recall B1 S03: the lowest knowing is to confess there is no god but He, with no likeness and no peer (al-Fath b. Yazid from Abu al-Hasan)". ask the scholar's repeat (JS P003:1-17, P004:2-30, including his note that this is the first thing to know about God) sits under the recall in the Teacher Copy; teach it again? It supports this lesson's thesis (oneness first) but is not the claim itself, so not kept as a core repeat.
  • SAME (recalled, not taught): U4.1-9 (Khoei's four pillars): "Recall B1 S14: Khoei counts belief in God, His oneness, prophethood and bodily resurrection as the four pillars without which one is outside Islam". ask the scholar's repeat (JS P019:2-P026:4, the Misbah al-Faqaha passage) sits under the recall in the Teacher Copy; teach it again? Step 6 stands on EZ P015 and P027 without it.
  • PART (base recalled, new part taught): U4.1-1: base "Recall B3 S01: oneness is treated right after proving the Maker"; new: oneness among the Quran's most important attributes (wahid, ahad and phrases with their meaning), in JS P001:4-9, P002:6-10.
  • PART: U4.1-3: base "Recall B3 S01: asked to summarise faith, the Prophet named the Lord's oneness first"; new: the Malik ibn Nuwayra setting and the full order (resurrection, prophethood, Ali's wilaya, obligations, abstaining from sin), in JS P005:3-15, P007:13-15, EZ P009:10-15, P010:9-15.
  • PART: U4.1-7: base "Recall B1 S09: tawhid is central in the Quran"; new: tawhid defined as ascribing oneness to the proven self-subsisting Creator and believing it, in JS P017:3-8.
  • PART: U4.1-8: base "Recall B3 S01: al-Rida, God is made one and not divided"; new: oneness as both a positive and a negative attribute, in JS P018:1-2.
  • PART: U4.1-13: base "Recall B2 S06: the prophets did not come mainly to argue for God's existence; existence and oneness are separate questions (B3 S01)"; new: the axis of their work is tawhid, making people know and worship the One, in EZ P038:2-5, P039:2-4, P040:4-6.
  • PART: U4.1-14 (held, Teacher Copy): base "Recall B1 S14: belief about God must not rest on others' say (Ibrahim's own witness against imitation)"; new: belief must rest on proof, the Pope analogy, in EZ P041:1-10.
  • PART: U4.1-16 (Teacher Copy): base "Recall B1 S13: felicity depends on the basic roots (God, Prophet, Imams, resurrection)"; new: the testimony formula and the five-item list, in JS P043:3-10.
  • PART: U4.1-19: base "Recall B1 S10: knowing God is required even without practical need"; new: the question applied to oneness, left open, in EZ P046:13-19.
  • PART: U4.3-9 (Teacher Copy): base "Recall B1 S03 and B1 S10: the minimum is confessing no god but Him, and God makes Himself known"; new: Kitab Sulaym, Ali on the least by which one is a believer, in MM P026:3, P026:8-9.
  • Core repeat kept in full: none.

The shared-Arabic table lists it as inline in B3 S18 only; B1 S03's block was missed because it starts at «بأنه». Printed with the SAME note under the recall.

  • Inline only there, printed normally: «الله لا اله الا هو» and 2:255 (inline in B1 S12, B1 S13). If the U4.3-9 pointer is widened to MM's whole span, 48:4 «أنزل السكينة في قلوب المؤمنين» is already printed in full in B1 S16 and gets the flag; as a pointer it prints only the Kitab Sulaym line.
Who carries it

JS and EZ. AH and MM are absent from this Fasl (0 spans in both parts; MM only in the U4.3-9 Teacher Copy pointer). AH enters only through the re-read records: RR7-005 (the dua «اللهم عرفني نفسك», step 5, Handout layer); RR10-003 (2:138, "the dye of God", possible illustration at step 3, uncertain fit); RR1-006, RR1-011, RR10-002 (fine, likely other Babs).

Imports

none proposed in draft 1 (no imports/H01.tsv); none kept, none dropped.

Teacher Copy only

U4.1-9 (now a SAME recall, see above), U4.1-16 (PART, one line plus the new formula), U4.3-9 (MM, Kitab Sulaym, pointer at step 4; «فيقز» corrected to «فيقر»); the fine RR records. Pointers to MM U4.7-3 and U4.7-20 at step 5 (in-Bab). Points moved out to other lessons, as draft 1: U4.1-4, -17, -18 to H10; U4.1-5 to S02; U4.1-10 to H18; U4.1-11 to H23; U4.1-15 to H24; U4.1-20 to H20; U4.1-21, -22, -23 to H16.

Held out

U4.1-14 (taqlid and the Pope; method, not oneness; names the lecturer), proposed for Bab 1 marifat or Bab 18 manhaj. Under rule 7 a later Bab does not borrow back into Bab 1, so if it leaves Bab 4 it can only go forward (Bab 18). Stays in this Bab's Teacher Copy, flagged ASK.

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. ﴿قل هو الله احد﴾ 112:1 and ﴿وإلهكم إله واحد لا إله إلا هو الرحمن الرحيم﴾ 2:163 (or 18:110 in its mushaf wording): ayat, not in the Jami check. Both flagged as already printed in full (B2 S01, B2 S09; B3 S05).
  2. «أدنى المعرفة ... الإقرار بأنه لا إله غيره ولا شبه له ولا نظير»: FOUND, al-Kafi 1:141 (Kafi 1:86 standard), al-Tawhid, Uyun via Mustadrak Safinat al-Bihar 7:179-180; one report of al-Fath b. Yazid from «أبي الحسن». JS's «لا شبيه له، لا نظير له» is FOUND-OTHER-WORDING (another text, «وحد المعرفة...»). Now under the SAME recall, flagged as already printed in full in B1 S03.
  3. «علمني الإيمان» (Malik b. Nuwayra): FOUND-OTHER-WORDING, al-Fada'il (Shadhan) 1:192-196, Bihar 30:343, Ithbat al-hudat 3:200, late works only; Jami's list is the two testimonies, the pillars and wilaya, with no resurrection.
  4. ﴿إن الدين عند الله الإسلام﴾ 3:19, first clause only: an ayah; flagged as already printed in full in B2 S03.
  5. ﴿إن الله لا يغفر أن يشرك به﴾ 4:48, alone: an ayah; flagged as already printed in full in B2 S10.
  6. «اللهم عرفني نفسك ...» (RR7-005; al-Sadiq to Zurara, al-Ghayba 2:88): FOUND; flagged as already printed in full in B1 S10.
  7. Teacher Copy pointer U4.3-9 (MM P026): Kitab Sulaym «أدنى ما يكون به مؤمنا أن يعرفه الله نفسه فيقر له بالربوبية والوحدانية», FOUND-OTHER-WORDING, Kitab Sulaym 59 and Bihar 66:17 («فيقز» a misprint). «هو الإيمان» on 48:4 is al-Sadiq's in al-Kafi, not al-Baqir's.
Next lesson picks up

if oneness is the axis of the prophets' work, with what did God send them, and what did they all say? (S02)

S02 ہر نبی ایک ہی دعوت لایا: اللہ کے سوا کوئی معبود نہیں، اور دین اپنے اصولوں میں ایک ہے

draft 1: H02 · 11 new · 9 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 3 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. What does the Quran say it was sent for? That people know He is One God (14:52), be reminded of the Lord and called to worship Him alone; jinn and men were made to worship, and the chief worship of prophets and Imams is grafting tawhid into hearts. (MM H1 P001:1-10, P002:1-8, P004:1-5; AH P053:2-20; U4.3-1, -3, U4.29-19). [U4.3-1 PART: recall B1 S09 "the Quran first wants the Lord known; tawhid is its chief teaching", then new: 14:52 and the Book's aim named as knowing His oneness, in MM P001:8, P002:8 | U4.3-3 PART: recall B1 S09 "the Quran is a reminder of the Lord and His signs", then new: read in order of revelation it is the call to worship Him and the ban on other gods, in MM P004:1-5 | U4.29-19 kept in full (rule 7: B1 S02 borrowed it from Bab 4); base also in B1 S16 and B1 S02 (bringing people to know God is the highest worship; 51:56), new in AH P053:3-20]
  2. Who is the first witness to this oneness? God Himself, then the angels and the people of knowledge (3:18); He does not lay down a law He stands outside. (MM H1 P003:9, P003:25; U4.3-2, U4.30-3). new
  3. Why did God send prophets? (MM H1 P008:1-6; U4.3-4; TC U4.3-17). [U4.3-4 SAME: recall only, "Recall B1 S09: the Prophet's sermon (al-Tawhid): God sent prophets so that servants know their Lord by His lordship after denying and own Him one after resisting"; MM's repeat under the recall, flagged ASK | U4.3-17 NEW (Teacher Copy): Ali (al-Ihtijaj), God bound people with a proof addressed to show His uniqueness and oneness]
  4. What did He take from every prophet before sending him? Servanthood, oneness, the casting off of rivals. (MM H1 P015:1, P015:5-7; AH P012:2-4; JS H1 P017:10-13 one line; U4.3-5, U4.5-4). Next lesson picks up

if every prophet brought this one call, how did it play out among real peoples and kings? (S03)

S03 انبیاء نے ہر جگہ، قید خانے سے دربار تک، پہلے توحید پہنچائی اور احکام اس کے بعد آئے

draft 1: H03 · 13 new · 6 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Where did prophets call to tawhid? Everywhere, even in prison: before interpreting the dreams Yusuf asked «أأرباب متفرقون خير أم الله الواحد القهار»; the believer keeps this aim in every gathering. (JS H1 P029:1-P031:14, EZ P032:1-P033:28; U4.3-12; TC import U11.12/3). ↺ B3 S07 · B2 S06Already taught: B3 S07 "Yusuf with his cellmates (12:39), for muraqaba and teaching" and B2 S06 "12:39 as a reminder to the fitrah",New here: the call to tawhid came before the dream reading, and the aim kept in every gathering, in JS P030:7-13, P031:8-14, EZ P033:16-28
  2. How did Ibrahim face his people and Nimrod? My Lord gives life and death; you and your gods are not gods; summoned by Nimrod, he declared he worships the One; idols that cannot act (the three readings of «إليه», Teacher Copy). (JS H2 P034:1-11, P040:1-9; EZ P013:1-13; U4.3-13, U4.4-9; TC U4.3-18, U4.3-14). [U4.3-13 PART: recall B3 S19 "Abraham and Nimrod (2:258), for debate method and 'over God, not rule'", then new: Abraham's proclamation of oneness against the idols and Nimrod's claim, in JS P034:3-11 | U4.4-9 PART: same recall (B3 S19), then new: only Abraham's plain declaration that he worships the One, in EZ P013:2-13 | U4.3-18 NEW (Teacher Copy) | U4.3-14 SAME: recall only (Teacher Copy)]
  3. How did Musa begin with Pharaoh? From God, at a high level, with origin and return: «ربنا الذي أعطى كل شيء خلقه ثم هدى»; Pharaoh could keep his rule if he said la ilaha illa Allah; prophets begin with rulers; tawhid, prophethood and resurrection were taught before the tablets. (EZ H2 P047:6-P050:9; U4.3-23, -24; TC import U10.11.16). [U4.3-23 PART: recall B1 S09 and B3 S19 "Moses' mission was to turn Pharaoh to his Lord; over God, not rule", then new: keep the rule but confess God; prophets begin with rulers, in EZ P047:20-30, P048:1-17 | U4.3-24 PART: recall B1 S09 and B1 S10 "beliefs are taught before rulings (said of the Prophet)", then new: Moses had taught tawhid, prophethood and resurrection before the mount, in EZ P050:1-9]
  4. How did Christ's envoys call in Antioch? Healing on faith in the One God; the crowd's «لا إله إلا الله»; the man who came running (36:13-22). (EZ H2 P036-P037; U4.3-15). new
  5. How did Yusuf bring all Egypt to say it? "As you are toward me, so am I toward God: worship my God, not me", said willingly. (EZ H3 P059:2-11, P063:7; U4.3-27, -30). new
  6. Did the call build a society? Not under Ishaq and Isma'il; Yusuf's rule a sample without the laws; Musa and 'Isa did not reach a full shari'a and society; the Prophet did, through great suffering. (EZ H3 P052:6-12, P054-P055, P066:5-P067:21; U4.3-25, -26, -27). new
  7. What did the Prophet call the People of the Book to? Ibrahim's millat and the common word; to Najran "in the name of the God of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob". (EZ H3 P070-P074; U4.3-35, -36; TC import U11.13/8). new

Who carries it

EZ and JS. AH and MM are absent from this part of U4.3 (H3 is EZ alone); AH and MM appear only through the kept imports below, Teacher Copy only.

Imports 5
  • U11.12/3 (Bab 11, AH): Nuh called his people 950 years (29:14) to believe in the unseen God and throw away Wadd, Suwa', Yaghuth, Ya'uq and Nasr. KEPT at step 1. Claude's judgement step 1 asks where prophets called to tawhid; Nuh's lifelong call to his people to drop their named gods for the One is the same call in another setting, so it supports the step directly, not only as a second voice.
  • U10.11.16 (Bab 10, AH): Musa answered Pharaoh "our Lord is He who gave each thing its creation, then guided it" (20:50). KEPT at step 3, simple line only, not the murabbi reading. Claude's judgement step 3 says Musa began with Pharaoh from God and origin; AH's reading of the very verse (my Lord is the One who made me and guides me) states that opening in his own words, so it supports the step's claim.
  • U11.13/8 (Bab 11, MM, AH, JS): Nahj 1, when the Prophet came people were in scattered religions, likening God to creation, deviating in His name or pointing to another, and God guided them through him. KEPT at step 7, simple sentence only. Claude's judgement step 7 asks what the Prophet called the People of the Book and the mushrikin to; Ali's description of the scattered religions he found and of their being guided back through him gives the state the common-word call answered, so it supports the step.
  • U11.13/7 (Bab 11, MM, AH, JS): Nahj 147, God sent Muhammad to bring His servants out of the worship of idols into His worship. DROPPED: no sound framing. Step 6 claims the Prophet built a society on tawhid where earlier prophets did not; Nahj 147 states the aim of his sending, not that a society was built, so it would only add a voice. Its natural fit is S05's question (his first call), where AH already carries.
  • Ten candidates withdrawn in draft 1 stay withdrawn (imports/H03-withdrawn.tsv).

Next lesson picks up

was mankind on tawhid from the start, how did shirk come after it, and who kept Abraham's teaching alive until the Prophet? (S04)

S04 توحید پہلے آئی اور شرک بعد میں: پہلا انسان نبی اور موحد تھا، اور ملتِ ابراہیم علیہ السلام کے حنیف پیرو جاہلیت میں بھی پیغمبر صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ تک باقی رہے

draft 1: H04+H05, folded · 15 new · 12 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 4 kept in full
The steps one claim followed through: tawhid first, shirk a fall, the remnant never lost until the Prophet renewed it 13
  1. What do the histories of religion teach about where tawhid began? Fear of nature, spirits, fetish, many gods, two, then one with Abraham; whoever takes that scheme must date tawhid from Abraham and make Adam and Noah symbols. (EZ H1 P014:1-53, P016:1-21, P010:1-21; U4.4-10, -11, -6). [U4.4-10 SAME: recall only | U4.4-6 SAME: recall only | U4.4-11 PART: recall B3 S19 and B2 S03 "the evolutionary account refuted; Adam and Noah held tawhid before Abraham", then new: the scheme forces tawhid to start with Abraham and turns Adam and Noah into symbols, in EZ P016:5-21]
  2. Is it true? No: the first man was a prophet, Safwat Allah, the first Muslim; no prophet held many gods; the religion with God is Islam (3:19); Adam's first prostration was worship of God alone with honour to His friends. (EZ H1 P001:11-15, P002:5-30, P003:5-26, P004:1-32, P005:2-10; MM U4.7 H2 P041:6-8 one line; U4.4-1, -2, U4.7-26). [U4.4-1 and U4.4-2 kept in full (rule 7: B2 S03 borrowed them from Bab 4; U4.4-2 is also this lesson's core claim) | U4.7-26 NEW]
  3. Then why is Islam tied to Abraham? Only the name and the written milla are his: from the day God made man his religion was Islam; Noah came before him, and from Adam through Seth and Idris the essentials reached Noah; Abraham was no Jew, Judaism is named after Judah, and Moses first called to Abraham's hanif shari'a. (EZ H2 P017:2-31, P018:1-12 with 22:78, P024:1-4; H1 P006:1-14, P007:1-11; U4.4-3, -4, -12; TC U4.4-15). [U4.4-12 SAME, kept in full as a core repeat | U4.4-3 kept in full (rule 7: B2 S03 borrowed it) | U4.4-4 PART: recall B2 S03 "Abraham's religion is Islam, never abrogated", then new: Judaism named after Judah, Moses first called to Abraham's hanif shari'a, in EZ P007:1-11 | U4.4-15 NEW (Teacher Copy)]
  4. Do the prophets climb from alphabet to doctorate? No: beliefs are one, conveyed by degrees; the tawhid Abraham taught is more perfect than today's. (EZ H2 P022:7-12, P023:3-18; U4.4-14). ↺ B1 S09Already taught: B1 S09 "no difference among prophets in beliefs about God; each begins where his people stand",New here: the school-grades image refuted by name, in EZ P022:10-12, P023:4-5
  5. What is the hanif religion? Turning from every falsehood to the One (6:79); janif the opposite; the orientalists misread the hunafa; pure tawhid became the hanif religion the Prophet followed (16:123). (EZ H1 P009:4-22, P011:1-13, P008:10-15; U4.4-5, -7). [U4.4-5 NEW | U4.4-7 PART: recall B3 S16 and B3 S19 "6:76-79 for the proof from origination and «وجهت وجهي»", then new: that pure tawhid became the hanif religion the Prophet followed, in EZ P011:6-13, P009:9-22]
  6. Did Mecca begin with idols? No: Gabriel showed Adam the Ka'ba's site; it was the first house of tawhid (3:96); Abraham conveyed prayer, fasting, hajj, imamate, wilaya, tawhid and the Resurrection, his line stayed monotheist, and idols came later as regression. (EZ H2 P019:1-11, P020:1-9, P021:7-15, P027:15-17, P031:1-13, P032:1-3; H1 P012:4 one line; U4.4-13, -19; TC U4.4-16, -17, -8). [U4.4-13 PART: recall B2 S03 "tawhid came first; religions did not evolve from shirk; Abraham's line kept it", then new: the same shown for Mecca, in EZ P019:3-11, P020:3-9 | U4.4-19 PART: recall B2 S03 "Abraham's descendants down to the Prophet kept tawhid", then new: the list of what Abraham conveyed and the Arabs' drift for lack of a preacher, in EZ P031:1-13 | U4.4-16, -17, -8 NEW (Teacher Copy)]
  7. What became of Abraham's teaching among the Arabs? Kept but distorted for want of a prophet: a yearly Abrahamic hajj, worship turned into whistling and clapping (8:35); Islam corrects it, it does not copy the Jahiliyya. (EZ H2 P021:2-5, P028:1-17, P032:4-7; H3 P037:4-8 with 9:36, P057:1-4; U4.4-18; TC U4.4-24; TC import U11.12/14). [U4.4-18 PART: recall B2 S03 "Abraham's line in Mecca (Qusayy, Abd Manaf, Hashim) kept his tawhid", then new: the hanif religion current across Arabia with Mecca its centre, and the distortion of worship, in EZ P028:1-17 | U4.4-24 NEW (Teacher Copy)]
  8. Who kept it? The hunafa, rooted in Abraham's dua for a muslim nation (2:128); the name is Abraham's title, not "nobles" or "Christians"; Hashim, Abd al-Muttalib, Abu Talib, Abd Allah; tribes around Medina who needed neither Moses nor Jesus. (EZ H3 P042:1-23, P044:11-19, P047:1-13, P057:1-18; U4.4-26, -31; TC import U11.12/13). [U4.4-26 PART: recall B2 S03 "Hashim and Abraham's line kept his tawhid", then new: hanif as Abraham's title, the named hunafa, Abd al-Muttalib's open call, in EZ P044:11-19, P047:1-13 | U4.4-31 PART: recall B2 S03 "a family of Abraham's descendants kept his religion", then new: tribes around Medina as hanifs, outside the missions of Moses and Jesus, in EZ P042:18-23, P057:1-18]
  9. Were the Prophet's ancestors among them? Yes: "your moving among those who prostrate" (26:219); Abd al-Muttalib before Abraha, who came because the Ka'ba was the seat of tawhid; Kinana trusted God, owner of the House. (EZ H3 P038:2-P041:9; H2 P033:1-21, §4; U4.4-25, -20, -21; TC imports U10.18.16, U15.22/1). [U4.4-25 PART (Teacher Copy for the extension): recall B2 S03 "God kept the Prophet's line on Abraham's tawhid", then new: all ancestors from Adam to Abd Allah and Ali's to Abu Talib, legatees like Abd al-Muttalib, and the proof from 26:219, in EZ P038:4-9, P041:1-9 | U4.4-20 PART: recall B3 S12 "the Elephant army as a proof of God's power", then new: Abraha came because he saw the Ka'ba as the seat of tawhid, to prove the House had no Lord, in EZ P033:1-21 | U4.4-21 NEW]
  10. Did the region know the coming Prophet? Sayf ibn Dhi Yazan: he will break the idols, put out the fires and worship al-Rahman. (EZ H2 P035 and H3 P036 merged, one quote; U4.4-22, -23). new
  11. Did every hanif believe at once? No: Khadija, Khuwaylid and Fatima bint Asad were hunafa and Khadija believed at once; Abu'l-As, Aqil and al-Abbas came late; Khawla's tribe and the Banu Hanifa stayed apart. (EZ H3 P050:1-3, P051:4-6, P053:12-21, P056:4-16; U4.4-28, -29, -30, -27, -32). [U4.4-28, -29, -32 NEW | U4.4-27 NEW (Teacher Copy) | U4.4-30 PART: recall B2 S03 "the Prophet's ancestors kept Abraham's tawhid", then new: Abu'l-As and many early Muslims as hanifs, in EZ P056:4-16]
  12. What trace did it leave? Labid's half-line "everything but God is void", which the Prophet praised. (EZ H3 P061:1-2, P062:9-13, P063:4-8; U4.4-33). new
  13. Close: the Prophet came saying "I follow the milla of Abraham the hanif" (16:123): the tawhid that came first was renewed, not invented. Bridge to S05. (EZ H3 P057:8)

  • If printed: 2:213 «كان الناس أمة واحدة» (B1 S09) if U4.3 P008:4-6 comes here (Ask 15); 3:191 «ربنا ما خلقت هذا باطلا» (B1 S05, B3 S07) if Ibn Mas'ud's remark at EZ P062:14-16 prints it. Each gets the flag if it comes in.

Who carries it

EZ alone in the built text of all three parts (AH 0, MM 0, JS 0 spans). MM has one line moved in (U4.7-26). AH and MM reach the lesson only through the kept Teacher Copy imports below (all in the H05 half). Under the standing rule (AH and MM carry; EZ and JS add) this lesson has no AH or MM carrier in its Handout; see Ask 3.

Imports 6
  • U11.12/14 (Bab 11, AH): out of tribal pride each tribe made its own god, 360 idols round the Ka'ba, a god of pounded dates eaten in famine. KEPT at step 7. Claude's judgement step 7 claims Abraham's teaching survived among the Arabs but was distorted; AH's picture of the idols around Abraham's own House shows how deep that distortion went, so it supports the "distorted" half of the step. It does not support the "kept" half.
  • U11.12/13 (Bab 11, AH): before the mission every tribe but the Banu Hashim, who kept Abraham's creed, worshipped idols. KEPT at step 8, the Banu Hashim clause only. Claude's judgement step 8 asks who kept Abraham's religion; AH names the Banu Hashim as its keepers, which is the step's answer in his words. His "no trace of divine knowledge in Arabia" is narrower than EZ's hunafa across Arabia (Khawla's tribe, the Banu Hanifa); the tension is flagged, not resolved.
  • U10.18.16 (Bab 10, AH): Abd al-Muttalib told Abraha "I own the camels, the House has an Owner". KEPT at step 9. Claude's judgement AH tells it for reliance on God; step 9 reads the same words as the ancestor's faith that the Lord of the House, not its idols, would defend it. The words name one Owner of the House, so the framing is sound, though it is not AH's own point.
  • U15.22/1 (Bab 15, MM, AH, EZ): the Prophet's light passed from Adam through pure loins and wombs untouched by the filth of shirk («ولا يصيبنا نجس الشرك»), so no ancestor of the Prophet and the Imams was a polytheist. KEPT at step 9, the simple claim only; the light creation stays in Bab 15. Claude's judgement step 9 claims the Prophet's ancestors were monotheists; MM's narration and AH's line state exactly that, so the passage supports the step itself.
  • U11.12/8 (Bab 11, AH): on the night of the Prophet's birth the Persian fire went out and every idol fell on its face. DROPPED: no sound framing. Step 10 asks whether the region knew of the coming Prophet; a birth wonder is not anyone's knowledge of him, nor a sign that Abraham's religion lived among the Arabs. It matches Sayf's «يكسر الأوثان» only in motif.
  • The ten withdrawn in draft 1 (imports/H05-withdrawn.tsv) and the six removed from H04 on 2026-11-18 (U1.1.13, U11.12/16, U11.12/3, U11.13/9, UN2.3.2, UN2.5.1) stay out. Under rule 5 the Bab 1 and 2 ones among them (U1.1.13, UN2.3.2, UN2.5.1) could not come back in any case.

Next lesson picks up

the Prophet came with Abraham's milla; with what words did his own first call begin, and why? (S05)

S05 «رسول اللہ صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ کی پہلی پکار توحید تھی: لا الہ الا اللہ کی گواہی اور انداد کو چھوڑنا، اور اسی پر آپ آخر تک ڈٹے رہے»

draft 1: H06 · 9 new · 8 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 6 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Why did the call begin with tawhid? Awakening to the One known by fitra is realised by calling to worship, and its first, most upright instance is the spoken shahada; nothing goes before it; the call was not gradual. (AH H1 P001:1-13; EZ H1 P003:1-6, P004:1-12, P005:1-16, P007:1-23 one line; U4.5-1). [U4.5-1 SAME, kept in full (rule 7: B1 S02 and B2 S07 borrowed it from Bab 4; it is also this lesson's core claim)]
  2. What were the words, and where? After «فاصدع بما تؤمر», at the Hijr: «أدعوكم إلى عبادة الله وخلع الأنداد والأصنام ... وشهادة أن لا إله إلا الله»; casting off rivals means no rival, likeness or equal (112:4). (AH H1 P010:1-10, P011:1-6 restored, P012:2-3; EZ H1 P002:1-17, P006:5-10, P007:12-20 one line; U4.5-3). [U4.5-3 SAME, kept in full (rule 7: B2 S07 borrowed it from Bab 4; core)]
  3. What did he ask in the market and of his kin? «قولوا لا إله إلا الله تفلحوا», the one word asked of Quraysh (al-Kafi); at Yawm al-Dar he set out three principles (tawhid, his prophethood, Ali's wilaya), called the clan "to the oneness of God" and swore "by God besides whom there is no god, I am God's messenger to you"; since his kin were mostly monotheists, the axis with them was his messengership and the Resurrection. (AH H1 P008:1-12, P009:1-2; EZ H2 P032:2-7, P033:2-22, P034:1-3, H1 P021:1-11; U4.5-2, -11, -17, -18). [U4.5-2 kept in full (rule 7: B2 S07 borrowed it); base also in B1 S09 (U1.4.18, U1.4.20, U1.4.21) | U4.5-11 PART: recall B1 S09 "his call began with tawhid from the first day", then new: Yawm al-Dar and its three principles, in EZ P021:6-11 | U4.5-17 PART: recall B1 S09 "his early call was to the oneness of God", then new: the Yawm al-Dar wording and the oath, in EZ P032:2-7, P034:2 | U4.5-18 PART: recall B1 S09 "a prophet begins where his people stand (Shu'ayb)" and B2 S03 "his kin kept Abraham's tawhid", then new: so his axis with his kin was messengership and resurrection, not tawhid, in EZ P033:2-22, P034:1-3]
  4. Why did Quraysh refuse the one word? Making the gods one God was "a strange thing" to them (38:5). (EZ H1 P020:1-24; AH H1 P008:4-5 in Urdu; U4.5-10; JS U4.6 H1 P013:1-6, U4.6-10). [U4.5-10 PART: recall B2 S07 and B1 S09 "the one word of the call was la ilaha illa Allah", then new: the idolaters' reaction, 38:5, in EZ P020:1-24 | U4.6-10 SAME: recall only]
  5. Did he bend when they offered wealth, wives and rule? No: "say your Allah and we our idols, only do not say la ilaha" was refused; sun and moon in his hands would not move him. (EZ H2 P025:6-13, P025:40-47, P026:1-14; EZ U4.6 H3 P060; U4.5-14, U4.6-41). new
  6. How did he call: force, lure or a claim to rule? None: only the worship of the One God, to kin, Mecca and Medina alike; Medina won by 6:151, not by wealth, sword or class agitation; Meccan suras on belief and the plain call of 3:64; he asked nothing for himself and never said "I am God". (EZ H1 P015:1-15, P016:1-13, P017:1-24; H2 P035:1-8, P036:1-5, P037:1-27, P029:2-9, P038:1-2; U4.5-7, -19, -20; TC U4.5-12, -15, -16, -21, -22, -23, -25). [U4.5-7 kept in full (rule 7: B2 S07 borrowed it); base also in B1 S09 (U1.4.42, U1.4.22) | U4.5-19 PART: recall B1 S09 "the Prophet made no claim to rule; his axis was knowing God", then new: the Meccan suras and the plain call of 3:64 without war, in EZ P036:1-5, P037:1-27 | U4.5-20 NEW | Teacher Copy: U4.5-12 PART, U4.5-22 PART, U4.5-25 PART, U4.5-15, -16, -21 NEW, U4.5-23 SAME: recall only]
  7. How did the call prevail? Badr: "if this band perishes You will not be worshipped"; Hubal answered «الله أعلى وأجل»; at the conquest «لا إله إلا الله وحده وحده وحده أنجز وعده». (EZ H1 P018:1-10, H2 P043:2-9; AH H1 P013:1-10; U4.5-8, -24, -5). [U4.5-8 NEW | U4.5-24 NEW | U4.5-5 kept in full (rule 7: B2 S07 borrowed it); B2 S03 uses the same conquest sermon for another teaching ('you are all from Adam')]
  8. What is left of it? The pilgrims still say «لا شريك لك» (one line, full in H18); Muslims confess the One several times a day with Surat al-Ikhlas; 9:33 came true. (AH H1 P014:1-6, P024:1; EZ H2 P045:7-8; U4.5-26 TC, U4.5-13 TC). [U4.5-26 NEW (Teacher Copy) | U4.5-13 SAME, kept in full in the Teacher Copy (rule 7: B2 S07 borrowed it)]

Next lesson picks up

the call was "say la ilaha illa Allah"; what does the word itself confess? (S06)

2. The word and the testimony S06 to S10

S06 «لا الٰہ الا اللہ» زبان کا اقرار ہے کہ اُس ایک ذات کے سوا کوئی معبودِ برحق نہیں، جو گنتی اور حد سے پاک ہے

draft 1: H07 · 16 new · 8 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. After the Prophet's call, what is the first duty? To confess by the tongue what one was awakened to: no hidden, exalted One before whom creatures are humbled but the Essence known by fitra; «قولوا لا إله إلا الله تفلحوا» as a one-line recap of S05. (AH P001:1-11; U4.6-1). ↺ B2 S07 · B1 S09Already taught: B2 S07 and B1 S09 "after the fitri call the first duty is to say 'no god but God'",New here: what the word confesses, the hidden exalted One known by fitra, in AH P001:4-11
  2. How does the word stand to «الله أكبر», and how much does it hold? Akbar is tawhid in summary, the shahada is the same made explicit; the word "Allah" set in it is a summary chapter of thousands of chapters; «لا إله إلا هو» is the Greatest Name and pillar of tawhid, with 3:18. (AH P002:1-19, P003:1-4, P005; U4.6-2, -3, -5). [U4.6-2 PART: recall B3 S01 and B1 S09 "Akbar means greater than to be described; Allahu akbar holds a world of knowledge", then new: Allahu akbar as tawhid in summary and the shahada as tawhid made explicit, in AH P002:3-19 | U4.6-3 PART: recall B1 S09 "a condensed word unfolds into many chapters (said of Allahu akbar)", then new: the same said of the word "Allah" in the shahada, in AH P003:1-4 | U4.6-5 NEW]
  3. What exactly does the «لا» negate? The genus: no god whatsoever but Allah; the idols, Pharaoh, Jesus are not gods. (EZ P044, P045 after a grammar check; U4.6-31). new
  4. Does it deny that anything else exists? No: creatures plainly exist, but as pure need subsisting by another, while He alone is rich (35:15); it denies a rightful god, "no god worshipped other than Him", to whom belong creation and command (7:54); "Allah" names the Essence gathering all perfections, ilah the rightful object of worship (114:3). (JS P006:6-28, P007:2-23, P016, P021:2-16; EZ P019:6-12; U4.6-6, -12, -14, -25). [U4.6-6 PART: recall B2 S09 "the fitrah is against identity of God and creation" (and B3 S08: 35:15 for the route from need), then new: la ilaha illa Allah does not mean nothing exists but God; creatures exist as pure need, in JS P006:13-28, P007:2-23 | U4.6-12, -14, -25 NEW]
  5. Is it then only the rejection of idols? No: it confesses real, non-numerical oneness, the Creator's non-determination in every way, against the grammarians' reading; the idols' negation is included and lower; the same «لا ... الا» runs through power, knowledge and life. (AH P025:1-14, P026:4-8; U4.6-18, -19). new
  6. Where does this confession live? In the heart and in thought as well as on the tongue: «كلمة جعل الإخلاص تأويلها وضمن القلوب موصولها وأنار في الفكرة معقولها»; tawhid of the heart is confessing that the Essence is never determined outwardly, in imagination or in reason (6:102-103; detail left to S08). (EZ P048:1-9, P049:1-7; AH P014:1-19; U4.6-34, -35, -11). [U4.6-34 PART: recall B1 S12 "Fatima's Fadak sermon opens with lofty tawhid teaching", then new: its line that ikhlas is the interpretation of la ilaha illa Allah, tied to hearts and thought, in EZ P048:7-9, P049:1-7 | U4.6-35 NEW | U4.6-11 PART: recall B1 S03 "casting off rivals means tearing the veils of imagination and reason" and B1 S13 "faith is confession by tongue and knowledge in the heart", then new: tawhid of the heart defined as confessing the Essence is never determined (6:103), in AH P014:1-7, P014:14-19]
  7. What do the narrations call this word? Kalimat al-ikhlas (lam-alif), kalimat al-taqwa, «حقاً حقا», God's proof through the messengers, the good word of the rooted tree (14:24, with Muhammad rasul Allah and Ali wali Allah). One narration per name. (JS P036:2-24, P037-P038, P040:6-8, P041:23-25, P042:5-9, P043:3-7; EZ P046:10-21; U4.6-28, -30, -32, -29, -27). [U4.6-28, -30, -32, -29 NEW | U4.6-27 PART: recall B2 S03 "tawhid read as the full three-part testimony (la ilaha illa Allah, Muhammad rasul Allah, Ali wali Allah)", then new: applied to the good word and rooted tree of 14:24, in JS P036:2-13, P037:7-17, P038:6-14]

Next lesson picks up

if the word confesses the One, why must its «لا» be said before its «الا»? (S07)

S07 توحید «اللہ اللہ» کہنے سے نہیں بنتی: پہلے «لا الٰہ» سے ہر جھوٹے خدا کی نفی، پھر «الا اللہ» کا اثبات، اور اس کلمے پر کوئی سودا نہیں

draft 1: H08 · 9 new · 4 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 6
  1. Is saying «Allah, Allah» tawhid? No: unless «لا إله» first shows the idols powerless and man-made, Allah, Lat, Uzza and Manat can stand together; the Quraysh had «Allah» on their tongues. (EZ P050:1-23, P051:14-16; U4.6-36). ↺ B2 S13Already taught: B2 S13 "the polytheists acknowledged Allah alongside their idols",New here: one is no muwahhid until «لا إله» dethrones the idols before «إلا الله», in EZ P050:2-23, P051:14-16
  2. Why must the negation come first? The farmer clears bad roots before sowing; the heart has no room for others, the angel enters when the demon leaves; the sword of «لا» (Rumi, one line, attributed); one «لا» in a lifetime (Farazdaq's verse on Imam Sajjad). (EZ P056:5-7, P057:14-16; AH P028:1; EZ P023:8-16; U4.6-38, -21, -16). new
  3. What does «وحده لا شريك له» claim for God? He alone owns essentially; Prophet, Imams and angels hold on loan by His grant, so none may say "I"; kibriya' is His mantle; two kings do not fit one land; His the kingdom and the praise. (AH P004:1-14 with 59:23; JS P009:1; AH P029:1 one line; U4.6-4, -7; TC U4.6-22). [U4.6-4 PART: recall B2 S09 and B3 S14 "existence and perfections are God's, given in measure from outside", then new: none, not even the Prophet or the Imams, owns essentially or may say "I"; «الكبرياء ردائي»; two kings in one land, in AH P004:2-14 | U4.6-7 NEW | U4.6-22 NEW (Teacher Copy)]
  4. Can the word be bargained down? No peace between Islam and kufr, though Muslims and kafirs may sign a peace: «لا إله» negates kufr, «إلا الله» affirms God, and neither half can be dropped, so at Hudaybiya the message was not given up. (EZ P059:1-24, P053:1-19; U4.6-40). ↺ B1 S15Already taught: B1 S15 "outward peace and unity with others does not make their way true",New here: the two halves of the word against kufr, and Hudaybiya, in EZ P059:2-24, P053:5-19
  5. Does "negation first" reach beyond the idols of Mecca? EZ extends it: disavowal before love, tabarri before tawalli, first curse then greet (Ziyarat Ashura), knowing the oppressors of Karbala and the Saqifa. (EZ P051:1-23, P054:1-20, P055:1-20, P056:8, P058; U4.6-37, -39). [U4.6-37 PART: recall B1 S15 "against mystic pluralism: Abraham broke the idols; 'love's house is everywhere' is wrong", then new: disavowal before love, la ilaha before illa Allah, tabarri before tawalli, in EZ P054:1-20, P055:1-20 | U4.6-39 NEW]. Draft 1 kept this step only if Ali rules it in; under rule 8 it stays in the plan, flagged ASK (Ask 3).
  6. Close: the word in the second person in dua, «أنت الله الذي لا إله إلا أنت»; a word of three letters (lam, alif, ha) said without moving the lips, heavy in the balance with al-hamdu lillah. (JS P031:5-19, P032; U4.6-24; TC U4.6-23). [U4.6-24 NEW | U4.6-23 NEW (Teacher Copy)]

  2. The tabarri run (step 5: la'n before salam, Ziyarat Ashura, Saqifa and Karbala): keep here, move to H16 or H24, or leave out?

Next lesson picks up

where does the confession live: is the tongue enough without the heart's knowledge? (S08)

S08 توحید کی گواہی وہی سچی ہے جس میں دل کی معرفت اور زبان کا اقرار ایک ہوں، اور جس کا اخلاص انسان کو حرام سے روک دے

draft 1: H09 · 17 new · 3 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. What does a person witness to in «أشهد أن لا إله إلا الله»? The tongue confesses what the spirit already sees of His holiness, and the servant witnesses God's own witness to His oneness; the Hijr call is recalled in one line from S05 (H06), its home.
    • U4.7-1. ↺ B1 S13 · B2 S08Already taught: B1 S13 "a true testimony issues from the heart's knowledge, not from the tongue alone" (also B2 S08)New here: the spoken testimony confesses what the spirit witnesses of His holiness and exaltation above any like or partner, seeing it in His meeting, in MM H1 P001:7; with JS H1 P003:1-9, P005:1-3 (Nahj 101 «يوافق فيها السر الإعلان والقلب واللسان»)
    • U4.7-10. ↺ B1 S04 · B1 S10Already taught: B1 S04 "God is His own proof and witness, and the servant finds Him" (also B1 S10)New here: the servant's testimony to oneness is his finding (wijdan) of God's own testimony to Himself, and so is itself the denial of every rival, in MM H1 P001:7 (second half)
  2. Is saying it enough? No: witnessing is valid only with the heart's knowledge, «لا تجوز إلا بمعرفة القلب»; the hearsay and the eyewitness.
    • U4.7-9 (JS H1 P019:3-16; EZ H1 P020:3-29, with «أشهدك يا مولاي» P020:9). [CORE REPEAT, kept in full: B1 S13 already teaches this from the same Bihar 81:132 text; core repeat · ask, Ask 14]
  3. Where does that knowledge come from? Reason, supported by light, makes oneness sincere; Iblis was given all of Ayyub but his intellect, left «ليوحّد الله به»; and the word is too evident to be hidden from any fitra.
    • U4.7-2 (MM H1 P006:1, cut to the ikhlas clause; reason told to speak: «الحمد لله الذي ليس له ضد»). [NEW per packet; see Ask 16: B2 S06 prints Kafi 1:69 in full and draws the same lesson]
    • U4.7-19 (MM H2 P033:7). new
    • U4.7-17 (MM H2 P031:3, one line). new
  4. If the heart knows, must the tongue speak? Yes: iqrar is the lowest knowing; Pharaoh knew and pride kept him from confessing, and his confession at drowning did not avail.
    • U4.7-8 (JS H1 P015:1-15; EZ H1 P016:1-22). ↺ B1 S13recall only, "Recall B1 S13: knowing is not enough without iqrar; Pharaoh and the obstinate knew and did not confess"
    • U4.7-38 (JS H3 P064, with 10:90, 40:85, 4:18 one line). new
  5. What should the testimony be like? Heart and tongue agreeing, its sincerity tested and its core believed, a faith whose sincerity negates shirk and whose certainty negates doubt, a testimony of iman, iqan, ikhlas and idh'an, to One with no equal.
    • U4.7-27 (JS H3 P044, from «وأشهد»). new
    • U4.7-30 (JS H3 P054:7). new
    • U4.7-31 (JS H3 P056:2). new
    • U4.7-32. ↺ B1 S13Already taught: B1 S13 "faith and testimony live in the heart and on the tongue"New here: sermon 195's four kinds, iman, iqan (belief at its highest), ikhlas, idh'an (confessed on the tongue as well as in the heart), in JS H3 P057:4-30, P058:2-8; the second reading of ikhlas (no added attributes, P057:29-30, P058:2-5) as a note pointing at Bab 5
    • U4.7-39 (JS H3 P065). new
  6. What is the sign of that sincerity? That the word holds one back from what God forbade.
    • U4.7-4 (MM H1 P010:1). new
    • U4.7-28, Teacher Copy (JS H3 P048, Jamal, one line, Ask 2). new
  7. Which testimony is honoured? One given knowing the truth (3:53); the servant calls God and all creation to witness and stores it for the terrors ahead; «بل إقراركم بتوحيدي أعظم» in one line (in full in S14); close on the youth who rises testifying.
    • U4.7-34 (JS H3 P060). new
    • U4.7-33 (JS H3 P059:5). new
    • U4.7-11 (JS H3 P044 s8-13). new
    • U4.7-24 (MM H2 P039:3-4). new
    • Teacher Copy: U4.7-7 (sermon 85, Ask 3) new; U4.7-18 (Shia scholars as «شهداء الله بتوحيده», Ask 4) new; U4.7-25 (the Baqir oath and Majlisi) new.

Next lesson picks up

What does the testimony on the tongue do in this world: who counts as a Muslim, and what takes him out? (S09)

S09 دو کلمے کہنے والا مسلمان ہے، اور یہی دو کلمے کفر اور اسلام کی سرحد ہیں جو جان، مال اور عزت کی حفاظت کرتے ہیں

draft 1: H10 · 12 new · 9 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. What makes a person a Muslim? The two testimonies, freely said on the tongue, nothing more; pure Islam is the testimony of oneness; outward Islam rests on the two words, and both rest on accepting God's divinity.
    • U4.7-12. ↺ B2 S01Already taught: B2 S01 "all in the heavens and the earth submit to Him, willingly or not (3:83)"New here: within his freedom man is asked to confess the truth, and by saying «أشهد أن لا إله إلا الله» he submits and becomes Muslim, while earth and time are muslim by creation, in EZ H1 P023:1-18
    • U4.7-13 (EZ H1 P017:7-10, P018:1-8; 3:64 inline). new
    • U4.7-20. ↺ B2 S01Already taught: B2 S01 "a line of al-Rida's letter to al-Ma'mun, read there for the fitrah"New here: the letter's definition, pure Islam is the testimony of oneness, then the Book and the Imams, in MM H2 P034:2] [RULE 7, kept in full and counted as a core repeat: B1 S12 borrowed this point from Bab 4 as a flagged Teacher Copy import; Bab 4 is its home; core repeat · ask, Ask 16
    • U4.1-17. ↺ B1 S02 · B1 S10Already taught: B1 S02 "belief ranks above practice; one who holds the doctrines but is lax in acting is still a Muslim" (also B1 S10)New here: Islam's two stages, explanation and implementation, and what the first stage asks of every Muslim, in EZ U4.1 H2 P044:2-13; 33:39 out, Ask 4
  2. Then is every Muslim a believer? No: «قالت الأعراب آمنا» (49:14); faith needs the four beliefs and accepting the Prophet's judgement (4:65).
    • U4.7-3 (MM H1 P009:6; EZ H1 P017:1-2; EZ H2 P027:6-11). new
    • U4.1-4 (the same report, MM U4.1; in full here once, Ask 3). new
  3. How was entry into Islam done at the start? By the two words alone, rulings taught after: Khadija, Zayd, Addas at Ta'if, Hatim's daughter, and Ibn Salam.
    • U4.7-46 (EZ H4 P077:4-7). new
    • U4.7-47 (EZ H4 P079:7-11). new
    • U4.7-44. ↺ B1 S09 · B1 S02Already taught: B1 S09 "the call must begin with God's oneness, where the people stand" (also B1 S02)New here: the Addas story at Ta'if, Islam as two words, and "I am God's envoy, not God" as the same call Jesus made, in EZ H4 P071:4-34, P072:1-47, P073:1-29
    • U4.7-43. ↺ B1 S09 · B1 S10Already taught: B1 S09 "the Prophet's call starts with the testimony, beliefs before rulings" (also B1 S10)New here: the Prophet told Hatim's daughter that entering Islam is only saying the testimony, rulings come after, in EZ H4 P070:11-22, P074:5-9, P076:16-19
    • U4.7-45. ↺ B1 S09 · B2 S17Already taught: B1 S09 "oneness and casting off rivals come first, then the prophet" (also B2 S17)New here: her own account, his messengership not as a king, then prayer and right conduct, in EZ H4 P070:1-17, P074:3-4, P075:1-25, P076:1-15; the 16:90 part to S05, Ask 8
    • U4.7-23 (MM H2 P038:1-9). new
  4. Even from an enemy, or under the sword? Yes: Ikrima under amnesty; the two words are the border between kufr and Islam and protect life, property and honour, because God Himself honours them.
    • U4.7-49 (EZ H4 P081:10-12). new
    • U4.7-15. ↺ B1 S13Already taught: B1 S13 "outward Islam can stand without inner belief"New here: the two words as the border of kufr and Islam, binding even when said falsely or under the sword, protecting life, property and honour, and to be made one's dhikr, in EZ H2 P028:6-25, P029:4-15; Abu Hamza's «إن قوما آمنوا بألسنتهم» once, EZ H1 P024:4, Ask 10
  5. And if we are sure he lies? He is still treated as a Muslim: «أفلا شققت قلبه»; «لا تقولوا لمن ألقى إليكم السلام لست مؤمنا» (4:94).
    • U4.7-42. ↺ B1 S13Already taught: B1 S13 "outward Islam counts though the heart is unknown"New here: the Usama story, «أفلا شققت قلبه», one who says it may be kept from office but not killed, in EZ H4 P069:1-44, one version
    • U4.7-14. ↺ B1 S13Already taught: B1 S13 as above, one lineNew here: no ceremony beyond the two words, the Christian bride on the way to the marriage office, the suspected liar treated as Muslim unless he denies a necessity, in EZ H2 P026:3-30, P027:6-40
  6. What takes him out, and what does not? Denying a necessity of religion does; sin does not: a sinner stays a Muslim.
    • U4.7-16. ↺ B1 S02Already taught: B1 S02 "sins make one who accepts God and the Prophet a sinner, not an unbeliever"New here: the tongue-level testimony as the first level that makes one Muslim, and drinking or lying making him a sinful Muslim, not a kafir, in EZ H2 P030:4-11 (with P026:10-11, 21-25)
  7. Close: why value these words? God Himself confirms them from behind the veil, «صدق عبدي، أنا الله لا إله إلا أنا»; make them dhikr (back to U4.7-15).
    • U4.7-22 (MM H2 P036:28-29). new
    • Teacher Copy: U4.1-18 (Ziyarat Al Yasin, the testimony before the Imam wherever one lives, EZ U4.1) new; U4.7-48 (Abu al-As) new; U4.7-50 (the delegate) new; U4.7-51 (Imam Hasan and the Bedouin) new.

Next lesson picks up

if «لا إله إلا الله» is the border, can its opposite, "all are gods", also be true? (S10: truth is one)

S10 حق ایک ہے: لا الٰہ الا اللہ کے ساتھ نہ بت جمع ہو سکتے ہیں، نہ ہمہ خدائی، اور بندہ رب کے سامنے بندہ ہی رہتا ہے

draft 1: H11 · 3 new · 4 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Can two opposite claims about God both be true? No: «لا إله إلا الله» and "all are gods" cannot both stand; either the Prophet is right or the idolaters; Ibrahim's axe and 6:79.
    • U4.10-1. ↺ B1 S15Already taught: B1 S15 "truth is one: two opposite paths cannot both be right, and Ibrahim broke the idols"New here: the rule applied to «لا إله إلا الله» against the idolaters' "all are gods" and hama-khudai, with the Prophet's and Amir al-Mu'minin's breaking of idols, in EZ P001:14-20, P002:6-12, P003:11-18; the day-and-night example (P001:2-13) only as the one-line bridge from the recall; the sects and Hafez run (P003:1-10) Teacher Copy, Ask 5
  2. Is truth then relative to the person, the society or the age? No: fixed realities do not move with assent; one for you, three in Christendom, all things in India cannot all be true; «لا إله إلا الله» and Hubal cannot both be true.
    • U4.10-3. ↺ B1 S15 · B3 S19Already taught: B1 S15 "opposite claims cannot both be true (Ptolemy and Copernicus)" (also B3 S19)New here: the relativist's "God is one for you, three for them, all things for others, and all true" answered on tawhid itself, «لا إله إلا الله» and Hubal, in EZ P005:1-22
  3. How do we judge a religion or a founder, and did truth "evolve"? By the truth first: «لا يعرف الحق بالرجال، اعرف الحق تعرف أهله»; the truth is tawhid, not shirk in one age and dualism in the next; God alone is the real Truth; the dualist founder as test case.
    • U4.10-5. ↺ B2 S03 · B1 S09Already taught: B2 S03 "religion did not evolve from many gods to one; the first humans knew God" (also B1 S09)New here: «اعرف الحق تعرف أهله» as the measure of religions and founders, the truth being tawhid and God alone the Truth, in EZ P007:1-19; the Zoroaster test framed, without the greeting, Ask 9
  4. And if most of the world does not say the word? The majority is not the measure (6:116).
    • U4.10-6 (EZ P008:10-14, 27-32). new
  5. Does "tawhidi worldview" mean all is God? No: one God, Owner, Creator, Lord and Ruler; not the world as God and His manifestations; «إنا إليه راجعون» is return to His court, not becoming Him.
    • U4.10-2. ↺ B2 S09 · B1 S09 · B3 S14Already taught: B2 S09 "being is His light and His gift, not the Lord; the fitrah rejects making God and creation one" (also B1 S09, B3 S14)New here: the tawhidi worldview, right as one God, Owner, Creator, Lord and Ruler, wrong in the mystics' sense, in EZ P004:4-24; AH RR3-005 one line
  6. Then how do "I" and "You" stand? Both exist, as Lord and slave: the Gabriel report in its Jami wording; "I am I and You are You" as equals is shirk, "I am You" is kufr, the lesson's own explanation.
    • U4.10-7 (EZ P009:9-17, P010:21, P011:30-34, P014:17). new
  7. How does a slave speak to Him? The Kufa munajat: Master and slave, Rich and poor, Creator and created; close back on step 1, one truth, one Lord.
    • U4.10-4 (EZ P006:3-11). new

Next lesson picks up

if the truth about God is one, how does God describe Himself in His own words? (S11: Surat al-Ikhlas)

3. The sura, and nothing separates S11 to S13

S11 سورۂ اخلاص اللہ کا اپنا بیان کیا ہوا نسب نامہ ہے: وہ احد اور صمد ہے، نہ اس سے کچھ نکلا نہ وہ کسی چیز سے نکلا، اور کوئی اس کا ہمسر نہیں؛ اسی لیے یہ توحید کی کسوٹی ہے، اور جو اسے پڑھ کر اس پر ایمان لائے وہ توحید کو پہچان گیا

draft 1: H12+H13 · 17 new · 9 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 0 kept in full

Fold: the fold puts H13's "measure, proof, practice" after H12's verse-by-verse reading as its consequence: because the sura is God's own account of Himself (steps 1 to 6), nothing may go beyond it (7), it answers every false god (8), it proves the one who brought it (9), a seeker recognises it (10), and it is kept daily (11).

The steps 11
  1. What is this sura? God's own description of Himself: «فإنها نسبتي ونعتي», «كذلك الله ربي»; the sura shown once here in full; it sums up revelation's whole claim on tawhid; hence its names, Ikhlas and Tawhid.
    • U4.12-1. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the sura is called the Lord's lineage (nisba)"New here: the Mi'raj prayer report, «اقرأ قل هو الله أحد كما أنزلت فإنها نسبتي ونعتي», and the Prophet's «كذلك الله ربي», in MM H1 P001:1-6, P003:1-2; JS H1 P005:1-14, P006:3-26, P007:1-9, P008:1-11
    • U4.12-16. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the sura is the Quran's emphatic statement of who God is"New here: it summarises the whole claim of the school of revelation on tawhid, its reward and exegesis still to come, in JS H2 P047:4-8, P052:5-6
    • U4.12-18 (JS H2 P051:7-14). new
  2. Why was it sent? The Jews asked «انسب لنا ربك» and after three days it came; the unbelievers pointed to their sensed gods and asked for his; their own scripture had a god who wrestled.
    • U4.12-2. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the occasion: the Prophet was asked to describe his Lord, and the sura is His lineage"New here: the Jews as askers and the three days' wait, in MM H1 P010:6-9, JS H1 P008:13-16; AH H1 P011:1-7 (Genesis 32 framed as AH's wording, Ask 9); JS P013:4-6 restored, Ask 5
    • Import U11.11/8, kept, Teacher Copy only (see Imports).
  3. What do «هو» and «الله» say? He is beyond sense and reason; «الله» names the Essence every intellect is bewildered in; a God who gives birth is for midwives.
    • U4.12-10. ↺ B1 S05 · B1 S12Already taught: B1 S05 "the Essence bewilders intellects" and B1 S12 "such tawhid statements prove the mission"New here: AH's and JS's verse-by-verse gloss, «هو», «الله», no womb, no father, none equal, in AH H1 P023:1-4, JS H1 P024:1-23, P025:5-12, P026:29-34, P027:1-12, P028:1-15, P029:1-11, P030:1-15, EZ H1 P031:1-19; the midwives image Teacher Copy, Ask 7
    • Teacher Copy: U4.12-12. ↺ B1 S09 · B1 S12Already taught: B1 S09 "a short formula holds the whole of divine knowledge, Allahu akbar" (also B1 S12, the four tasbihat)New here: the nesting, all tawhid verses in the sura, the sura in «لا إله إلا الله», that in «الله», jam' and nashr told once, in EZ H2 P033:2-21, P034:3-6, P035:3-6, P036:5-22
    • Teacher Copy: U4.12-29 (EZ H2 P033) new; U4.12-22 (AH P064:1, the promise cut) new; U4.12-28 (the twenty questions as a teacher prompt, EZ H3 P070) new.
  4. What is «أحد»? The one all agree is one, and one in meaning, not one of a count.
    • U4.12-3 (MM H1 P010:1-5, P014:1-3). ↺ B2 S01recall only, "Recall B2 S01: al-Jawad, ahad is the One agreed upon in oneness (29:61)"
    • U4.12-4 (MM H1 P015:2, one line; AH RR6-001, RR6-003 Teacher Copy). new
  5. What is «الصمد»? Not hollow, the one everything turns to; God explained it by what follows; al-Baqir would draw the whole religion from it.
    • U4.12-13 (MM H1 P017:1, P037:3; JS H1 P012:10-11; MM P010:9-10 «ليس بمجوف», from U4.12-2). [NEW; B1 S12 borrowed it from Bab 4, so by rule 7 it is taught here in full]
  6. What do «لم يلد ولم يولد ولم يكن له كفوا أحد» deny? Nothing came out of Him, He came out of nothing, a son resembles his father and He resembles nothing, none is His peer. The full lam-yalid argument is S12.
    • U4.12-5 (MM H1 P016:2-3). new
    • U4.12-6 (MM H1 P017:1-3, the Husayn letter, in full here). new
    • U4.12-7 (MM H1 P017:1-3). new
    • (U4.12-10 above carries AH P023:2-3 here.)
  7. So why is it the measure? Do not pass what God said of Himself; it and the opening of al-Hadid were sent for the deep thinkers of the end time; whoever oversteps it errs.
    • U4.12-11 (JS H2 P032:8, Kazim). new
    • U4.12-15 (MM H1 P014:6, Sajjad, MM wording, 57:3 only). new
    • U4.12-14. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the sura is the Quran's statement of who God is"New here: it is the criterion (mi'yar) of tawhid questions, and whoever oversteps it errs, in JS H2 P038:15-25, P039:2-8, P045:7-16, P048:1-8; EZ H2 P040:27-40, P041:12-15, P042:1-20
    • Import U18.44/3, kept, Teacher Copy only (see Imports).
  8. How do four verses answer every false god? The idol, matter, the son and the dual god are all denied; the Quran corrects human ideas of God.
    • U4.12-21. ↺ B1 S09 · B1 S12Already taught: B1 S09 "the prophets came to correct beliefs about God" (also B1 S12, gods of wood and metal)New here: the sura's own attributes, ahad, samad, no begetting, no equal, as that correction against idol, stone, wood and man, in EZ H3 P063:1-7, told once (other tellings P040, P042, P053:9-13, P059:20 Teacher Copy)
  9. Why is it the Prophet's proof? An unschooled orphan brings the highest argument for tawhid in one line, in a rhyme on dal.
    • U4.12-26. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the sura's tawhid from an unlettered Prophet is divine knowledge"New here: the dal rhyme as one facet of the Quran's greatness, in EZ H3 P068:1-25, told once; AH P023:4 (U4.12-10) gives the proof line
  10. How clear is it to a seeker? Whoever reads it and believes it has known tawhid; the convert found in it the God his reason had grasped.
    • U4.12-17 (JS H2 P049:6-8, P050, Rida). new
    • U4.12-19 (JS H2 P054 to P056; EZ H2 P057 to P059). ↺ B1 S05 · B2 S07 · B3 S02recall only, "Recall B1 S05: the convert who found in Surat al-Ikhlas and 3:26 the God his reason knew" (also B2 S07, B3 S02)
  11. What does keeping it do? A sea of tawhid in two lines: a week without it, five prayers without it; forty effects; Amir al-Mu'minin's love of it.
    • U4.12-9. ↺ B1 S12Already taught: B1 S12 "the short sura holds the deepest points of tawhid"New here: the recitation narrations, a week without it is the death of Abu Lahab, five prayers without it, in AH H1 P019:1-21 (rizq story Ask 21), EZ H1 P022:3-10, JS H1 P020:1-7, P021:4-15
    • U4.12-20 (JS P061, P062). new
    • U4.12-23 (JS P065:7-15). new
    • Teacher Copy: U4.12-24 (one-doctrine sura, Qaf) new; U4.12-27 (Fusus and Japanese seekers, unnamed) new.
    • Held, in this Bab's Teacher Copy flagged: U4.12-25 (thaqalayn) new.

Who carries it

AH and MM carry steps 1 to 6 (U4.12 H1 is 57% AH+MM by spans: MM steps 1, 4, 5, 6; AH steps 2, 3, 6, 9 and 11), and MM the Sajjad wording in step 7. Steps 7 to 10 are otherwise JS and EZ; the two kept imports give MM a voice at steps 2 and 7. Re-read adds (AH): RR6-006 (step 1), RR6-001 and RR6-003 (step 4, Teacher Copy).

Imports 6
  • Kept: U11.11/8 (Bab 11, MM; al-Baqir: the unbelievers pointed to their sensed gods, "these are our gods", asked Muhammad to point to his, and the sura came down). Step 2, Teacher Copy only. Claude's judgement a second occasion report of the same sura; it shows the sura answering the demand for a god that can be pointed to, which is the step's question "why was it sent". Bab 11 keeps it in its Handout.
  • Kept: U18.44/3 (Bab 18, MM, JS; Amir al-Mu'minin: hold to what the Quran shows you of His attributes; God praised the firmly rooted for leaving the delving into what is veiled). Step 7, Teacher Copy only. Claude's judgement this is the same rule the Kazim hadith applies to the sura, "do not go beyond what God mentioned in His Book", given in Amir al-Mu'minin's words, so it supports the measure itself, not only adds MM's voice. Bab 18 keeps it in its Handout.
  • Dropped: U1.8.2 (Bab 1 import, rule 5).
  • Dropped: UN2.12.19 (Bab 2 import, rule 5).
  • Dropped: UN2.3.4 (Bab 2 import, rule 5).
  • Dropped: no sound framing: U11.11/9 (Bab 11, MM; nature, time, matter, man-made gods taken as lord). It lists false lords but says nothing of the sura, so it does not support the step's claim that the four verses answer them; it would only add MM's voice beside EZ.

Texts and Jami results 15
  1. 112:1-4, the whole sura, once at step 1 (EZ P031:4): ayah.
  2. «اقْرَأْ قُلْ هُوَ اللَّهُ أَحَدٌ كَمَا أَنْزَلْتُ فَإِنَّهَا نِسْبَتِي وَنَعْتِي» (Bihar 18:358), MM P001:5, with «كَذَلِكَ اللَّهُ رَبِّي» (Ilal 2), MM P003:1-2: FOUND (Bihar 18:358-359, 79:241, Nur al-Thaqalayn 5:707, Kanz al-Daqa'iq 7:331).

  6. «لَمْ يَلِدْ لِأَنَّ الْوَلَدَ يُشْبِهُ أَبَاهُ» (al-Sadiq; Bihar 3:304), MM P016:2-3: FOUND.
  7. «لَوْ وَجَدْتُ لِعِلْمِيَ الَّذِي آتَانِيَ اللَّهُ حَمَلَةً لَنَشَرْتُ التَّوْحِيدَ ...» (al-Baqir), MM P037:3, cut at «من الصمد»: FOUND (Mustadrak Safina 2:398, 6:376).
  8. «يَا أَبَا أَحْمَدَ لَا تَتَجَاوَزْ فِي التَّوْحِيدِ مَا ذَكَرَهُ اللَّهُ» (Kazim), Tawhid bab 2 h. 32, Jami wording: FOUND; Jami «لم يلد فيورث ولم يولد فيشارك».
  9. «إِنَّ اللَّهَ ... عَلِمَ أَنَّهُ يَكُونُ فِي آخِرِ الزَّمَانِ أَقْوَامٌ مُتَعَمِّقُونَ» (Sajjad), Kafi 1:91, MM wording, with 57:3: FOUND; «متعمقون».
  10. «كُلُّ مَنْ قَرَأَ قُلْ هُوَ اللَّهُ أَحَدٌ وَآمَنَ بِهَا فَقَدْ عَرَفَ التَّوْحِيدَ» (Rida): FOUND at Kafi 1:147 (Ask 19), continuing «كذلك الله ربي» (Ask 18).
  11. «مَنْ أَحَبَّ أَنْ يَخْرُجَ مِنَ الدُّنْيَا وَقَدْ تَخَلَّصَ مِنَ الذُّنُوبِ ... اثْنَيْ عَشَرَ مَرَّةً» (Faqih 1:212): FOUND-OTHER-WORDING (Ask 22).
  12. «آمَنَ وَأَمِنَ», JS P062:4: FOUND-OTHER-SPEAKER (Amali al-Saduq 432: Kazim reporting «بعض آبائي»; «صدق وغفر له») (Ask 17).
  13. 4:82 and 3:26 inline (3:26 flagged B1 S05, B2 S07 if printed); 57:3; Quran.
  14. Other span texts not printed as blocks: «ممسوس في ذات الله» (Ask 23); the week and five-prayer warnings (Ask 21); AH's rizq story; the funeral of 70,000 angels (Sa'd b. Mu'adh); «صرت محمد صاحب الجمع» (Ask 25).

Next lesson picks up

the sura says «لم يلد ولم يولد»; does that deny only a son, or every kind of separation from Him? (S12)

S12 اللہ سے کوئی چیز جدا نہیں ہوئی اور نہ وہ کسی چیز سے جدا ہوا، اس لیے مخلوق اس کی بنائی ہوئی ہے، اس کی اولاد نہیں

draft 1: H14 · 15 new · 11 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. What does the ayah deny at first sight? No son to inherit, no father to share His lordship, no consort, no partner in the kingdom, no equal; Humran puts the same creed before Imam Sadiq; the Trinity, three and one at once, falls with it.
    • U4.14-1 (AH H1 P001:2-3, 8-9, the Baqir gloss, printed from MM's copy U4.12 P037:4, Ask 1; JS H1 P002:20-28, P013:6-9). new
    • U4.14-2 (AH H1 P003:1-2, 17:111). new
    • U4.14-3 (AH H1 P004:4-7, 112:4). ↺ B1 S03Already taught: B1 S03 "the lowest knowing is to confess that there is no god but He, with no likeness and no peer"New here: AH's gloss of 112:4 with its full list, no kufw, mithl, shabih, 'idd, nidd, qarin or 'idl, in AH H1 P004:5-7
    • U4.14-4 (AH H1 P005:1-19, 31-47; RR6-004, RR6-007 in AH's voice). new
    • U4.7-40 (JS U4.7 H4 P066:1-23, the creed only; the oath and 42:11 to Bab 5, Ask 11). new
  2. Is it only bodily birth? No: the ayah denies every coming-out, dense or subtle, down to scent from a flower; so emanation, descent and the mystics' "gnostic birth" are birth again and are denied. The Husayn letter («لم يخرج منه شيء كثيف ... ولا لطيف») is recalled in one line from S11, where it is printed in full.
    • U4.14-5 (JS H1 P009:6-24, P010:4-10, P015:6-22, P016:1-7, P019:4-6, P020:2-10, P021:1-12, P028:5-9). new
  3. Does man then return into God? No: "return to your Lord" is not entering God to become part of Him.
    • U4.14-13 (EZ H2 P039:9-19; 89:27-30). new
  4. Why can nothing separate from Him?
    • (a) It would share and resemble Him and inherit His perfections: Imam Sadiq. U4.14-6 (JS H1 P027:8, 26-44; P022:8-11). new
    • (b) What sheds a part has changed, and what changes is needy; so nothing exudes from Him, He brings into being. U4.14-7. ↺ B3 S08Already taught: B3 S08 "the Qayyum who holds every changing thing does not change, and is neither born of anything nor is anything born of Him"New here: separation itself is change, so nothing exudes from God, He makes by insha' and ijad, and "rashh" is a poet's word outside the truth, in JS H1 P028:14-21 and P029:1-14 (with the book line «و ظاهر أن الرش يستلزم التغير»)
    • (c) Sermon 186: "He did not beget so as to be born, nor was born so as to be limited; exalted above taking sons and touching women". U4.12-8 (MM U4.12 H1 P018:1-7, MM's copy carries the block). new U4.14-10 (JS H1 P035:1-5). ↺ B3 S04Already taught: B3 S04 "imaginations, intellects and senses do not reach His Essence"New here: the sermon's clauses on not begetting, not being born, and being exalted above sons and women, in JS H1 P035:1-5; the no-change clause P035:10-13 to Bab 5, Ask 3
    • (d) What can shed could itself have been shed: Bihar's variant of the same sermon. U4.14-9, Teacher Copy. ↺ B3 S16Already taught: B3 S16 "what came to be has a beginning, and its Maker cannot be like it"New here: Bihar's variant and the reasoning that if anything could separate from Him He too could have separated from something, and if born He would have a beginning, in JS H1 P034:1-4, 13-28
    • (e) "He did not beget so as to be born" is a real argument, not induction: against Ibn Maytham. U4.14-24 (JS H2 P051:1-38), Teacher Copy. new
  5. And why is He not from anything?
    • (a) Being from something means He was not and then was: originated, an effect needing a maker; so "lam yulad" is itself the proof that He is azali and abadi, as reason too rules. U4.14-8. ↺ B3 S16Already taught: B3 S16 "what changes was not and then was, and the chain of originated things ends at an azali Creator for whom 'since when' has no sense"New here: reading «لم يولد» as establishing that eternity, in JS H1 P030:6-11, P032:1-3, P031:1-2, P033:2-3; Nahj 185 «الدال على قدمه بحدوث خلقه» (P031:3-4) printed again, flagged; P030:1-5 «لو كان من شيء لكان محدثا» as argument, not narration, Ask 17
    • (b) Sermon 182 (181 in Ibn Maytham's order): "not born so as to share in might, nor begetting so as to be inherited from and perish; no time preceded Him". U4.14-17. ↺ B3 S04 · B3 S09 · B3 S16Already taught: B3 S04 (also B3 S09, B3 S16) "the same sermon 182: He became manifest to intellects by the signs of firm governance, and increase and decrease do not alternate on Him"New here: the sermon's clauses on not born, not begetting and no time before Him, in JS H2 P043:1-13, P044:2-4
    • (c) Sharing the begetter's might makes each of the two composed of a shared and a distinguishing part, and the composed is needy. U4.14-18. ↺ B3 S03Already taught: B3 S03 "composition is need, and the needy is made"New here: begetting, however subtle, entails sharing might (absolute self-sufficiency), so composition, in JS H2 P044:5-22, P045:12-32
    • (d) This is a demonstration, not rhetoric for common minds. U4.14-19. ↺ B3 S03Already taught: B3 S03 as in (c), one line onlyNew here: the common "child resembles father" reading rejected and the sentence read as a strong proof, in JS H2 P046:2-11
    • (e) Teacher Copy around (b): U4.14-20, a student asks why might and not origination (JS H2 P047:1-16) new; U4.14-21, «موروثا هالكا» in common understanding (JS H2 P044:23, P048:4-29, P049:2-9) new; U4.14-22, copies that reverse lam yalid and lam yulad, Majlisi in Mir'at al-'Uqul (JS H2 P049:10-33) new; U4.14-23, "mawruth" as remnant, an origination reading (JS H2 P049:34-37, P050:2-14) new.
    • (f) He subsists by Himself: no kin, tribe, mother or father. U4.14-14 (AH H2 P040:1-2, Nizami's verse, Ask 8). new U4.14-15, Teacher Copy. ↺ B1 S06 · B3 S08Already taught: B1 S06 "we all subsist by You" and B3 S08 "everything needs Him and He needs nothing"New here: «لم يولد» read as God not separating from anything, and the term wajib al-wujud, in JS H2 P041:1-11, 19-22, with the Persian verse P041:12-13
  6. How do creatures stand to Him? He was not from anything and made what is from nothing, apart from things by His power and things apart from Him; so creation is His making, not His birth, and no creature, Prophet, Ali or anyone else, is God.
    • U4.14-11. ↺ B3 S16Already taught: B3 S16 "the same sermon: He was not from a thing, so His originating points to His eternity"New here: the sermon read for His oneness and samadiyya and «قدرة بان بها من الأشياء وبانت الأشياء منه», in JS H1 P036:2-13, P021:17-18
    • U4.14-12. ↺ B2 S09 · B3 S03Already taught: B2 S09 "being is His light and His gift, not the Lord" and B3 S03 "the sign is neither the Maker nor like Him"New here: "creation is not God's zayish", no creature is one with God, neither the Prophet nor Ali nor Shimr, flower nor thorn, in EZ H1 P037:19-30, P038:1-16 (the Dhi'lib text for EZ's garbled P038:4, Ask 12); the first mabna with 2:156 (P037:7-17) stays with U4.10
  7. And "there is nothing but Him"? Also wrong: it denies creation itself.
    • U4.14-16 (JS H2 P042:2-20, without names, Ask 6). new

Next lesson picks up

nothing came out of Him and He came out of nothing; but could there be a second god beside Him? (S13)

S13 اگر اللہ کا کوئی شریک ہوتا تو اس کے رسول آتے، اس کی بادشاہی کے آثار دکھتے اور اس کے افعال پہچانے جاتے؛ ان میں سے کچھ نہیں، اس لیے اللہ ایک ہی معبود ہے

draft 1: H15 · 17 new · 2 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 3 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Is there a proof of oneness in the Ma'sumin's own words? One sentence of Letter 31 to Imam Hasan, holding three proofs; as far as searched, only Amir al-Mu'minin gives it.
    • U4.22-4 (JS H1 P010:2, P023:4-7). new
    • U4.22-8 (JS «جہاں تک جستجو کی گئی», Ask 2). new
    • U4.22-9. new
  2. What would a second god have to be? The Essence with every perfection: wise, so he sends messengers; powerful and generous, so he leaves traces; with acts and attributes that show.
    • U4.22-1. ↺ B1 S16Already taught: B1 S16 "God's wisdom and grace require sending messengers"New here: a god as the Essence gathering all perfections, free of defect, whose power leaves traces and whose acts (creating, annihilating) and attributes (mercy, wrath) would be known, the premises of the Letter 31 proof, in JS H1 P001:10-22, P002:2-6, P015:6-13 and EZ H1 P003:3-6] [RULE 7, kept in full and counted as a core repeat: B2 S09 borrowed this point from Bab 4; core repeat · ask, Ask 14
  3. Did any messenger come from another god? No; the first mark is missing.
    • U4.22-5 (JS H1 P009:6-15; pointer to U4.3, S02). new
  4. And the traces and the acts? None show; what we see points to one Effecter, «ولكنه إله واحد كما وصف نفسه، لا يضاده في ملكه أحد».
    • U4.22-6 (JS H1 P019:5-12). new
    • U4.22-10 (JS H1 P030). new
    • U4.22-11. ↺ B3 S17 · B3 S03Already taught: B3 S17 "the harmony of the traces shows one governor" (also B3 S03, every creature says He is One without partner)New here: the principle that where the effect proving a specific cause is absent the cause is absent, applied to the messengers and the traces, in JS H1 P031:2-26, P029:12-13, P032:23-25
    • U4.22-20 (JS H2 P055:8-11). new
    • Import U4.20-5, kept, Teacher Copy only (see Imports).
  5. Could a second god exist and stay hidden? Stopped, he is weak; holding back because the work was done, he is outdone and the prophets would be liars; sharing unannounced is weakness, neglect or a lie.
    • U4.22-7 (JS H1 P022:3-4, P033:26-38). new
    • U4.22-12 (JS H1 P034:10-27). new
    • U4.22-13 (JS H1 P040:8-9). new
    • Teacher Copy: U4.22-14 (prophets split between two gods) new; U4.22-16 (the commentary's "persuasive" objection, book named, not the commentator) new; U4.22-17 and U4.22-18 (student exchanges) new; U4.22-19 (Tawdih al-Murad, Urdu meaning only) new.
  6. Is it circular to prove oneness through prophets? No: prophecy rests on God's existence, not His oneness; the prophets came to announce oneness, not to prove existence, «أفي الله شك».
    • U4.22-15 (JS H1 P044:4-19). new
    • U4.22-3. ↺ B2 S07 · B1 S09Already taught: B2 S07 "the prophets did not call people to accept that God exists but to 'no god but God'" (also B1 S09)New here: oneness follows from introducing God and from the interrelation of the world's parts, as a gathering cannot be without its one preacher, in EZ H1 P005:1-14, 20-23 (with 14:10)] [RULE 7, kept in full and counted as a core repeat: B2 S09 borrowed it; core repeat · ask, Ask 14
  7. Does the heart say the same? Reason without prejudice finds no two gods and says «أشهد أن لا إله إلا الله»; who knows a majlis knows its one preacher.
    • U4.22-2. ↺ B1 S16Already taught: B1 S16 "after knowing God, reason sees it needs a messenger to learn its duties"New here: the sincere seeker anywhere, setting prejudice aside, finds there cannot be two gods and «أشهد أن لا إله إلا الله» rises from his heart, in EZ H1 P004:12-27] [RULE 7, kept in full and counted as a core repeat: B2 S09 borrowed it; core repeat · ask, Ask 14
    • U4.22-21 (EZ H1 P005:10-14, H2 P056:5-25). new
    • Import U4.20-4, kept, Teacher Copy only (see Imports).
  8. Close: one pot, basil and mint, same water and soil, each different: has it added to your tawhid?
    • U4.22-22. ↺ B3 S09 · B3 S15Already taught: B3 S09 "the same causes yielding diverse effects point to a Maker" (also B3 S15, the Differentiator, the unseen Dyer)New here: the basil and mint pot and its framing as growth in tawhid, in EZ H2 P057:1-17 (red fruits out, Ask 7)
    • Import U4.27-16, kept, Teacher Copy only (see Imports).

  • Kept: U4.20-5 (Bab 4 M2, AH: a little reflection shows the world has one higher Director and Manager, 41:53). Step 4, Teacher Copy only. Claude's judgement what we see points to one Director, which is the positive side of the step "the traces point to one Effecter"; AH reaches it from the world's order, not from the missing traces of a second god, so the fit is partial. U4.20 keeps it in its own Handout.
  • Kept: U4.27-16 (Bab 4 M2, AH: among hundreds in the mosque no two faces, nails or eyes are alike though all grew from one air and soil; in everything a sign that He is One). Close, Teacher Copy only. U4.27 keeps it in its own Handout.
